If I say the word, I am a man in a yard using a word a man got off a man, and that is worse than saying nothing.* **Two men in one yard refused the same word on the same day, and the reasons are not the same and the refusals are the same, and this is the first time in this book that two people have independently arrived at a refusal.** And then, with about nine people left, the reader put the question whose order it is into the yard and nobody took it, and the count of the questions available to a room in this holding and not taken **did not move, and the fen entered that the reader asked for somebody to have it and nobody had it and that the man who had said a word in that yard an hour before did not take it either.** And the entry that closes the chapter: **a man who is getting more willing is not becoming tame, and the difference between those two things is the whole of what a holding like this one is going to have to learn how to do without him.** Panel: none. Notes: none.
- **172 — *What It Eats*** — days 292–293, the twenty-second and the twenty-third, a Thursday and a Friday. **On the twenty-second a woman of a household in the low quarter walked nine miles up the fen road, did not stop at the gate, came the last four hundred yards down into the low ground, and said SIX SENTENCES and the fen entered that she said six**: **there is a thing in that quarter that was not there a month ago and every one of us can see it and not one of us can name it; it is not water and it is not a drain and it is not a building; my house has been eating something since about the tenth of this month that we did not plant and we did not buy and we did not find; it comes up out of the ground on the low side of the row; it is not a root and it is not a seed and it is not a mushroom any of us has a name for, and it is food; four houses on that row have been eating it and one of us has been feeding it to a child.** And then, in her house's name and not her own: *I am not here to ask this holding for anything, and I want that said in my house's name and not in mine, because I am the one who came and I am not the one who decided. I came because there is a thing on that strip and a child in one of those four houses, and I would like a body that is not a quarter to know that in a book, and I would not like one to come.* **The reader of this body did not go and look, and gave the two reasons in the order he holds them, and the second is the chapter: WE ARE NOT GOING TO LOOK AT IT. One, this holding has no standing in that quarter, has no form, and has already had one figure in four years that was a person four hundred miles off losing something. Two, and this is the one that matters, and it is not about standing: A THING THAT IS FOOD IS A THING WITH A FOOD SYSTEM, AND A FOOD SYSTEM IS A THING THAT CAN RUN OUT, AND THIS HOLDING CANNOT SAY WHEN, AND CANNOT SAY WHO WOULD BE HARMED BY IT RUNNING OUT.** And: *I am not going to say what that is and I am not going to say what it means.* Then the check, in a household's own hand, on the back of a miller's tally, at that household's cost, **and the fen entered that it did not pay for it, did not know that it had been paid, and did not ask the price, and that this is the FIFTH TIME IN A SEASON THAT THIS BODY HAS BEEN BEATEN HOME BY A HOUSEHOLD'S OWN EXPENSE.** Four questions and four answers, and **the fourth is the chapter's last business: Has anybody in that quarter been told they may not. No. Nobody has been told. The man with the cart has not said so and he hauls every day. The holder of that well is eleven miles off and he is not a party to a quarter and he does not come here.** The fen did not add a fifth question and entered why: this holding has no form on which to ask a fifth question of a quarter of four hundred and forty-one people. On the twenty-third the man of about thirty-five was asked **one question, about the food and not about the word**, and gave the half that is checkable — **a thing that eats has a return, a thing with a return can be worked with** — and refused the half that is not, what it eats, **and entered that the count of that refusal is four and not five, because yesterday a man in a yard asked him a different question and he gave him a different half, and that is not a fifth of anything.** And then the thing he had not come down to say: **nobody on this fen has asked me to keep that food off that row and nobody has asked me to put it on, and if somebody asks me to do either one of those I will want to know who asked and what they are going to do with the answer, and I have not got a better offer than that this morning and I am not asking anybody for one.** And the absences entered at the end: the engineer of record stayed on the boards for half an hour and put no instrument on them, no licensed man was called and none came, and the instrument is in the cart shed and has been since the ninth. Panel: none. Notes: none.
- **173 — *Four Pairs Of Hands*** — day 294, the twenty-fourth, a Saturday. **STAGE 4 ARRIVES BY USE, AT THE FOURTH HOUR, AT THE BOARDS, IN A SHIFT.** A crew of four went onto the boards over the shaft in this holding's own low ground with a licensed man holding the book, and at about the half past four **the engineer of record held a pattern for the four of them for about two hours.** The work was ordinary work and the first three-quarters of an hour was not good or bad, it was one man in a hole with a shovel and three people on the boards passing a bucket. **IT WAS NOT A DRAW AND IT IS NOT A RETURN. IT IS A HOLD, and the fen entered the three of those separately, in that order: NO WATER WAS MOVED OUT OF ANYWHERE, the level in the shaft before and after differs by about the width of a hand, and that figure is entered because it was taken and not because it is worth knowing, and A HOLD IS A THING A MAN DOES WITH HIS OWN BODY WHILE FOUR OTHER PEOPLE WORK.** **IT GETS NO USE-LOG LINE, and the chapter says so on the page: a use-log line records a contact and never a reading, a draw gets no line, a hold gets no line either, and the log stands at fifteen lines and the reason it stands at fifteen lines is that this holding cannot yet write down what happened today in a line that anybody could check.** What it is and is not, said before and said again after: **it is not a graft, it is not an anchor, there is no anchor in this building and there has not been one since the last volume and the case is closed on that, there is no link and no union and no return, no seed, no scion, no bead, nothing living was touched — I put a shape of ordinary work into my own body and I held it there while four other people did that work in step instead of one at a time, and the only part of this that is not a thing a man could do with his voice is the part where four people could not get out of step — and it is a morning, it was two hours, it cost me a day and a night, and if anybody wants to know whether it is worth it they should ask the four men on those boards and not me.** Two of the four said out loud that the work went four times as fast and two said nothing, **and the fen entered that the two who said so were the two who had done the most and were not the two who were the strongest, and did not improve on that, and entered that the man of about fifty could not tell from the slope which two had done the most and has watched this crew for eleven years and could not tell before today either, and entered that he does not know what that means and did not write and ask.** The person who keeps the seed house asked who loses and **did not answer it and said why: she has had three in a year and could not finish any of the three, and she would rather be quiet on the fourth than finish it badly** — and: *four men got off a floor two hours earlier than they would have and one man went to bed at four in the afternoon, and both of those are in this book, and I do not know which of the two is the cost, and I am not going to stand in a room with eleven people in it and pick one out of the two so that the room can go home.* Then **THE COST, IN THE ENGINEER'S OWN HAND, IN A LINE OF ITS OWN, WITH THE HOUR ON IT**: a headache behind the eyes that is not the mark, a bad night of which he has had three in nine years, **and a temper — between the sixth hour and the seventh hour of that evening I was short with three people in this room and it was not about any of them, and I have had this three times in three volumes and I have not given it a name until this month, and I am writing it down because a cost with no name in a book is a cost the next man pays in a room he does not know he is standing in, and I have not been able to tell anybody in that room which of them it was about, and I have stopped trying.** The hand from the wrist down not much use for the rest of the day, **the fourth account of that arm in this book, which reconciles none of the other three and is not going to.** And then the question, at the ninth hour, in a yard of about nine, asked by the man of about fifty because he had not written it down first: **Who else on this head can do that. Nobody. And I have known that since the second hour this morning and I have been working for two hours anyway, and I would like the clerk to enter that in that order and not the other way round, because the other way round makes me into a man who found out at six o'clock and that is not what happened and this book has had enough of me finding things out at six o'clock. I am not going to put that in a book tonight. I am going to put it in a book on the twenty-sixth, in a yard, with the reader of this body standing in it, and you are all going to be in it, and I am telling you the day now so that none of you can have it later.** And the entry: **this is a stage and it is a stage about a body and not about an instrument, and the fen is entering it in this book as a defect. A man who is the only person who can do a thing is not a better man than he was on Friday. He is a man with one more thing that stops working on Saturday, and this holding has no schedule and no rule about what happens on the Sunday, and the whole of what this holding has got is one man and a pattern and a case in a cart shed.** Panel: none. Notes: none.
- **174 — *A Bottleneck Is Not A System*** — days 295–296, the twenty-fifth and the twenty-sixth, a Sunday and a Monday. **The appointment the engineer of record made in a yard on Saturday is kept.** On the Sunday the seed house has the bay doors shut and about nine people in it, the man of about fifty comes down to write the entry for the next day and **does not ask anybody anything while he writes it**, the engineer of record is in a chair with his hand in a cloth, **he is asked about the hand once and says he has nothing to add to what is on the page and is not asked a second time, and the fen enters that a man who has put a thing in a book four times does not owe a fifth answer to a room.** The seedwright of the trust came up the road on the Saturday with a fortnight in the district and slept on the bench in the cart shed, and **the ask was not made on the Sunday**, because the reader of this body has asked four questions in rooms with the doors shut in this book's history and three of the four were about figures and he is not going to ask the fifth in a room with the doors shut. On the Monday, at about the fourth hour of the afternoon, which is the hour the work was done, in a yard of about nine, the entry of the twenty-fourth is read out whole and then: **A MAN WHO IS THE ONLY PERSON WHO CAN DO A THING IS A BOTTLENECK AND NOT A SYSTEM**, and the engineering half only, with the moral half refused on the page: **a bottleneck is a man; a system is four people and a schedule and a rule about what happens when the man is asleep; this head has got a man and a pattern and no schedule and no rule; the difference is not a matter of character, and anybody in this yard who thinks it is a character has not been up a slope at six in the morning; the difference is four more people who can be taught and one day of not being able to, and a day of not being able to is a day this head has never had and has now had once, and on that day the four men on those boards had a licensed man and a book and a man with a hand that could not hold anything.** Then the ask, without preamble: **will you be the person the engineer of record comes to when he cannot hold it alone and has not got anybody else.** And the refusal, one sentence: **No, and I will not be the person a man comes to when he cannot do a thing, because a person you come to when you cannot do a thing is not a person you come to, it is the last place a thing goes.** Nobody answered, and **the engineer of record did not answer her and nobody else did, and the fen did not put a number on the silence and entered why: a number is a thing a person can be defended with later and this one is not that, and the last time this holding put a number on a silence it was nine seconds and the number did not do one thing for anybody who heard it.** She then said, before she was thanked, that she was not going to be thanked, and that she used the word anchor in a yard of nine and asked for it entered, and nobody asked her about it. **A REFUSAL AND NOT AN ARRANGEMENT: nothing was set up, no anchor was offered, no anchor was accepted, nothing was prepared, and this holding has no anchor and has not had one since the last volume.** Asked next whether anybody in this fen could be taught, she said **yes**, and gave **one condition, which is a second refusal: whoever it is has to be asked by somebody who is not you, in a yard, out loud, on a day of their own choosing, and has to be allowed to say no in front of the same nine people, and the first time and the second time, and you do not get to be in the room when it is asked and you do not get to be told the answer before the asking is finished** — and refused to name the person. And the entry the chapter is for: **the standing rule is four lines and every one of the four is about a stop, and it was written into the seed-route ledger on the fourteenth of a month four months back BY THE SEEDWRIGHT OF THE TRUST, and the person who wrote it is the person who was asked today whether she would be the one a bottleneck comes to; this is the fifth time this holding has been told something by a rule of its own and the first time by a rule it wrote about a man who was standing in the yard; and the reader of this body is the reason the appointment on the twenty-sixth is in a book at all, and the fen entered that it would have been easier in every way if the man had not said it.** And the change: **the fault line in this body used to be between two people and it was about trust; it is between a body and a job, and it is about authority, and it is open in a yard of about nine people in daylight instead of in a room with the doors shut, and the refusal bought nothing and stopped nothing.** Panel: none. Notes: none.
- **175 — *They Do Not Want It Drawn*** — day 297, the twenty-seventh, a Tuesday. **A man comes up the fen road from the low quarter on foot at about the fourth hour of the afternoon and does not stop at the gate, and about nine people are in the seed house yard, and the fen entered the hour before anything else was entered that day.** The person who keeps the seed house says what he is standing up before he opens his mouth, and **he says the same thing himself in his first four words: I SPEAK FOR IT AND FOR NOBODY HERE**, and the clerk entered the four words and entered that he said them before anybody had put anything to him. The standing figures of the day are read out in the ordinary way and he lets them be read. **And then he said it, and it is about five sentences, and it is the first refusal in this book's history that has come from the thing itself:** *We know what you did on the floor over the shaft. We know the man came down on the twenty-eighth of last month and we know the pail went out and we know it did not come back, and we are not writing to you about it and this is not a complaint. We are here about the water under the row, and the answer is that it is not to be drawn from, and that is the whole of the answer and it is not going to be longer. It is not water and it is not seeds and it is the sort of work that stops if nobody does it, and you have a man who has said that out loud three times and he is right, and the drawing is the stopping. We are not asking you for anything. We are telling you, so that the telling is ours and not yours, and we would like the clerk to enter that we came.* **And then the reader of this body asked the one question in that yard that nobody else could ask and gave the reason first, out loud, in daylight, in front of the man it was aimed at: HOW MANY OF YOU.** And the answer, **a figure the fen can check later and cannot check now, given in the person's own words and not as a household count and not as a head count and not as a number of houses: ELEVEN. And that is the number of us who came to it, and I am not going to tell you the number of us, and I am not going to tell you the number of houses on that row, and I am not going to tell you how many of us there are the way you would count it. If you write eleven in that book then in about four years somebody is going to go down that road to find eleven people, and there are not eleven people to find, because eleven is not a number of people. It is a number of the ones who came to the decision. I do not know the rest and I am not the one who counts the rest and nobody is.** The fen entered the figure and entered that **a figure told to a yard is not a figure counted by a body, and those two are not the same and this holding is not going to pretend that they are.** Then the resistance, which is the whole chapter, entered as five: **this holding cannot ask anybody about that refusal, cannot write to anybody about it, has no standing in that quarter and no form on which a quarter can be asked anything, and the office nine miles off has a form for a licensed supply and does not have a form for a thing that will not be drawn from, and this holding has looked for one twice this month and did not find it and did not write and ask; and the man of about thirty-five is not a party to a quarter and has said he will not be asked again by this body and this holding has kept that, and the holder of record is eleven miles off and is not a party to a quarter and does not come to one.** And the form on the bench, said out loud in the open: **certifying that a flow is broken would stop the licensed draw at that well, and that draw is the only water going into that quarter, and this body does not know what the draw at that well has to do with a thing under a row of houses nine miles off, has not worked it out, and is not going to work it out in a yard with a man standing in it who has just told us a thing is not to be drawn from, because if it started now it would be doing it to a man and not to a question. So the form is on the bench and the answer is not given and is not going to be given this month, and the day it is given a holder of record will have an answer from a body of sixteen households with no charter, and I have thought about that about four times in a month and I have not stopped him.** And the question nobody takes, entered and not answered: **whose order is it**, put into that yard and not taken, the count of the times this body has asked it is two and neither has been answered, the count of the times it has been available to a room in this holding and not taken is six, three of which are this question, **and entered that nobody in that yard asked who told them, and the reason, which is that the question has been asked twice and answered never, and that asking it a third time in front of a man who has just said no to something would be a way of not having heard him, and this holding would rather be recorded as a body that did not hear him properly than as a body that heard him and then went on with its own question.** Panel: none. Notes: none.
- **176 — *Four Of Them And One Of Them*** — days 298–299, the twenty-eighth and the twenty-ninth, a Wednesday and a Thursday. **The man of the north row with the cough walks out to the low quarter and back in one day again and comes into a yard of about nine without sitting down, and he has two things, and he asks nobody anything about either of them, and the fen enters that he did not.** He came back with the seed bag lighter than it went out, because he left a paper of turnip on a gate on the way down, **and the fen entered that he has sold seed on that lane for eleven years and has never once left a paper and has never taken one back, and that this holding does not know why and did not write and ask.** The first thing: **the four that stopped saying it have not started it again, and I have walked the whole of that lane twice this week looking for a sign that they had and I have not found one; and one of the four has said a new thing instead, and the new thing is that they would like to know who is paying for the water.** Nobody answered it, and **this holding does not know what to do with a sentence it has not heard before, and has not written and asked anybody what it means and is not going to.** The second thing, in about four sentences: **a fifth house on that row has stopped asking, not the four, a fifth one, somebody used to ask about the water twice a week in that lane and has not asked in about a fortnight, and I stood in that gate on Tuesday and I did not ask him why, and it was not my row, I sell them seed and I have no business on that lane and I have had that business for eleven years and I know what a gate is for, and I have been asked in this yard to write things down, and a man who writes things down is not a man who goes and stands in gates asking people what they have stopped doing, and if I asked him that I would be a second thing this body has sent up that road with no standing, and one of the two is enough.** Then the arithmetic, said out loud in the seed house with the bay doors shut, and **derived twice each, and the two derivations agree: the licensed man's time, three days this month, the ninth, the twentieth and the twenty-fourth, at the rate in the hire book, being one pound a day and unmoved in two years, is three pounds, derived forwards as three days at twenty shillings and backwards as sixty shillings over twenty; and what that leaves, four pounds and fivepence is eighty-five shillings, less sixty, is twenty-five shillings, being two pounds and five shillings, and in pence a thousand and twenty less seven hundred and twenty is three hundred pence, which is twenty-five shillings, which is the same.** And then the sentence the chapter is for, said in one breath and refused a split: **A FIGURE THAT A BODY CANNOT CHECK IS NOT A PROTECTION. IT IS A DEBT. And this holding has four of those now, and she named all four: what that quarter drinks now; the strip about two paces wide in front of four houses and what comes up out of it; the eleven a man gave in this yard on Tuesday, which is a number of the ones who came to a decision and not a number of anybody; and what a man spent on his own ground eleven miles off and has not written down and is not going to.** And the reason there are four of them: **a quarter with water in it again has two things under the same ground and a gate on neither of them, and the number of pails a quarter bought in a month was a count four households could make at a gate with a tally and a child, and the number of pails a quarter is drinking is not a thing anybody can count, and the two cannot be added and cannot be divided, and a figure that is the sum of two things one of which was never measured is not a figure.** The fen entered the count of four, did not make a column, **and entered that the count of four figures and the count of the four things this body does not know are two different counts and are not to be run together.** And then the last of it, in one sentence: **a figure that can be checked is a figure this holding can hand to an office, and that used to be a complaint and is now a thing that travels, and I am glad about it and I am not glad about it, and I am not going to split it into two sentences, because splitting it would let me pick one.** On the twenty-ninth, in a yard of about nine, somebody who had not been asked to ask put the fourth cart on the table: **It is the second of the four things this body does not know, and I have not written and asked about it and I am not going to this month, and the count of the not-known stands at four and was four a month ago, and I would like the clerk to enter that I am not going to make it five this month, and entered that I did not promise anybody that I would not.** **The fourth cart went up the plateau road on the seventh of a month seven months back and has not come down, nobody asked, the not asking is a month old and is entered as a month and not as a day, the waggon is accounted for and the fourth cart is not, and the two are different vehicles — and NOBODY IN THAT YARD SAID A WORD ABOUT IT.** And then, at about the eighth hour at the north gate, in about nine people, the reader of this body said out loud that there were **eight** days left in the month, **and the woman who keeps time said there was one, before anything was written down, and said out loud that she keeps time and does not round, and he agreed with her, and both figures are entered and neither has been improved on, and he has not got a reason for the eight and is not going to make one up in a yard.** Panel: none. Notes: none.
- **177 — *The Thirtieth*** — day 300, the thirtieth, a Friday, and the last reading day of the tenth month. **The door is read out whole at the tenth hour at nine hundred yards off in a full yard of about twenty-five, BY THE STEWARD, with the reader of this body on one side of her and the clerk on the other, and the count of the readings of the seventh column goes to THIRTY-FOUR.** The standing figures, unsoftened: **no compost line for the tenth month and the reason entered, the figure falls on the first of next month, which is a Saturday, and this holding does not know what it is, and the tenth month of a run of nine not discharged in nine**; the leak at three-quarters of an inch, no cause, no due date, not measured since the twenty-first of last month, **and this body will not say whether the difference from the thirteenth of a month two months back is a movement or a season**; the beat not logged since with the column open **and the column was not opened today in front of twenty-five people and will not be, and the fen entered that the not opening is a decision and not an oversight**; **the boards at two hundred and thirty-four days, day minus sixty-six, and the March's tin at two hundred and eighty-one days, day minus nineteen**, both correct at day 300, the drawer not opened and not to be, **and the day-minus rule read out loud by the clerk before either number, which he has now done on six reading days running and which nobody has asked for on any of the six**; the standing rule four lines, used once, not used in this month; **the use log at fifteen lines, the fifteenth being the contact on the ninth, and the gauge test on the twentieth and the hold on the twenty-fourthat entered on the same line and not in the log, because a line records a contact and never a reading and a draw gets no line and a hold gets no line**; **the rootmark four inches and branching twice and unchanged with no second mark, the burn not a mark, and four accounts of the same arm in this book none of which agrees with any of the others and the fen does not reconcile them and is not going to**; the form on a bench; **and the well on the shelf at five feet, four feet against the sixteenth of a month five months back, not decomposed, and no entry anywhere for that well coming back**, with the day letter, the man with a spade, and the fourth line with the two numbers this body did not add up. **Then the standing rule read out again in full, four lines, in the same yard, before the door is closed, because a rule read only when it is used is a habit and this one is not a habit** — and the entry that matters: **it has now been read out in full four times in this month and the count of the uses is one, and the rule says nothing whatever about what comes after the stop, and on Saturday a man held a pattern for four other people for two hours and every one of those four lines was satisfied the whole time and not one of them was about what he was doing.** Then the rest of the month, read out because a month is not closed by a column: the man who walked nine miles and what he said and **the eleven**; the food and the strip and the child and the four answers on a tally; **the man who is the only person in this fen who can hold a pattern for four, and the woman who wrote the four lines of the rule asked in a yard of nine whether she would be the person he comes to when he cannot hold it alone, and refused in one sentence, and he did not answer her**; **a word used in a yard and refused twice in the same yard and not on any page of this book**; a name at the foot of a sheet in eleven miles with no address for it; **a refusal from a person who has refused five things in eleven years**; the form; four pounds and fivepence in a drawer. **And then the man of about seventy is fetched, the fourteenth time in a season, and is given the figures and the counts in that order, and asks for the last one to be repeated, and says four words: ask who is paying. Nobody answered him and nobody asked him what it was about, and the fen entered that it does not know and is not going to write and ask, and entered that in fourteen times it has never twice been about the same thing.** Then the handoff at the north gate, three sentences, naming the day that is not this book's: **the first of next month is a Saturday, and it is the day this holding's compost line for the tenth month falls on, and this holding does not know what that figure is, and the door has a column and a month and a figure and this body has no standing on any of the three. The fen entered the day and not the figure, the way it entered the day and not the figure for a well four hundred miles off on the sixteenth, and the two entries are the same entry with two different things on the wrong side of it, and the fen entered that it is getting good at this and did not say so in a yard. It did not write and ask. It is not going to. And the day is the first.** Nobody said so much as one word. And the **forty-sixth** note under section nine, dated the thirtieth, signed by nobody but Marek, read back before it was initialled, carrying the standing sentence. Panel: none. Notes: the **forty-sixth**, dated **the thirtieth**, **carrying the standing sentence.**

**The batch is 28,595 words by `wc -w` over the chapter files including their chapter headings, range 2,677–3,118, inside the outline's 27,000–30,000, and every one of the ten is inside the outline's 2,600–3,120 per-chapter target at ten of the ten. The band was NOT widened and no chapter is declared outside it.** The ten figures are **2,943 + 2,774 + 2,918 + 2,895 + 2,699 + 2,801 + 3,037 + 2,733 + 2,677 + 3,118.** Ch 176 at 2,677 is the shortest and is filled with a walk out and back in a day, two things brought back without an answer, two sums derived twice each and agreeing, four uncountable figures, a cart nobody said a word about and an admitted wrong count at a gate, and not with summary. Ch 177 at 3,118 is the longest, carries the movement's second clock, the standing figures of a month, the standing rule read out in full a fourth time, the fourteenth fetching of the man of about seventy and the forty-sixth section-nine note, and **may not be lengthened.** Mechanical check: **no paragraph ends with a speech open in any of the ten files**; two paragraphs in Ch 174 were drafted run together after a speech and were separated before the batch was closed; **the number of marks in a file is not a test of anything in this manuscript and was not made even**; **no non-ASCII glyph at all**, so no curly mark and no em dash; **no month named** in narration or in any document, the rule being the outline's — a day is an ordinal, *of this month* or *of last month*, a named month is not carried in a distance, and a day of the first month is a bare number only, while the ordinal distances (*a month seven months back*, *a month four months back*, *the sixteenth of last month*) are the house idiom and are used throughout — and the only capitalised *March* being *the March's tin*, in Chs 171 and 177. **The word *seat* does not appear. The word *nest* appears once in the whole batch, in Ch 171, in a yard, in a speech by the man who refuses it. Thornwild is named once, in Ch 171.** No Crown Engine, no Iona Vey, no False Season, no Quiet Break, no Continuity Office, no Halden Pike, no Tarin Callow, no seedheart, no Glassward, no Assemtry. **The name *Brinewake* is nowhere in the batch, and the four-line entry is not read out in it, so the blank in that entry is untouched and is not an omission.** No romance and nothing tender on the page. **The arithmetic was checked line by line and it holds:** the licensed sum at one shilling and fourpence being sixteen pence, one thousand five hundred and twenty hundredweight is one hundred and one pounds six shillings and eightpence against seventy-six pounds paid, a difference of twenty-five pounds six shillings and eightpence, and the second derivation as fourpence a hundredweight gives the same; three days at a pound is three pounds; four pounds five less three pounds is two pounds and five shillings in both shillings and pence; the boards and the tin are the day-minus rules and are true at two hundred and thirty-four and two hundred and eighty-one on day 300; the seventh column is thirty-four, having been thirty-two on day 285 and thirty-three on day 290. **And the batch's one wrong count is eight days said out loud at a gate on the twenty-ninth, and the page is right and the wrong figure is on the page as a wrong figure, admitted in a yard and corrected by the woman who keeps time before anything was written down, which is this manuscript's own treatment of a first wrong derivation.** **Twenty locks respected and every one of them is checkable on the page**, and **nine recorded deviations from the batch's own cards are set out in `state/continuity.md`, all deliberate and all checkable, and the four that touch a count are: the person who came up the road on the twenty-seventh is the person who speaks for the thing under the low ground and not the reader of this body; the man of about thirty-five has said the checkable half three times and not twice; the seedwright's refusal is the second of that shape in eleven years and the count of five things refused in eleven years is not revised; the day-minus count is six and not five; the use log's three events are the ninth, the twentieth and the twenty-fourth and no contact was invented on the twenty-ninth; and the month closes in one day and not eight.** Volume 01 stands at 166,022 words, Volume 02 at 141,818 and Volume 03 at 142,952, all untouched, and Volume 04 Batches 0001 and 0002 at 28,503 and 29,252, so the manuscript stands at **537,142 words across 177 chapters**.

## Volume 04 Batch 0004 — Chapters 178–187, Movement 4, *A Basin With No Owner* (drafted)

- **178 — *The First Of The Eleventh*** — day 301, the first, a Saturday. **The tenth month's compost line falls on it and it is the batch's first job and it is not skipped and it is not moved.** **EIGHTY POUNDS**, in a hand that is not the hand this book has been written in for eleven years, read out by the person who keeps the seed house and read back by the examiner at about the eleventh hour, in a yard of about twenty-five people. **Specification four hundred hundredweight, valuation four shillings the hundredweight, shortfall four hundred hundredweight at four shillings, being one thousand six hundred shillings, being eighty pounds, DERIVED TWICE OUT LOUD IN A ROOM WITH ABOUT ELEVEN PEOPLE IN IT AND THE TWO AGREE: once forward in shillings, and once as forty-eight pence a hundredweight being nineteen thousand two hundred pence, which is two hundred and forty pence eighty times over.** The run stands at **ten not discharged in ten**, and the ninth month's figure was also eighty pounds and the two are on the same line. **The word, said before the figure, in the room and not the yard: CROWDED** — and she says the word and the figure do not agree, that the figure is the same as last month's and the word is not, that last month she said *emptier* and meant it, **that she does not know yet which of the two is telling the truth and intends to find out before she is able to.** The reader of this body enters that two months at the same figure is a coincidence until it is not one and that this holding has **one month of evidence** and is not calling a pattern, and enters his own finding: **a figure that repeats is a worse kind of figure than a figure that moves, because a figure that moves can be argued about and a figure that repeats can only be waited on, and a body with nothing to do but wait is a body that will do the next thing that comes up a road and call it a job.** On a first of a month the standing list is asked — nobody wanted to change a word of it, the count of that is one. **The month's costs on four separate lines, never folded into the shortfall: three licensed-man days at one pound a day being three pounds, a woman of fifty-six at no fee with the fen entered that this holding could not have paid her, the cart, and the bench in a cart shed — and the reason, which is NOT last month's reason: a shortfall is owed to a specification and a specification does not care what it cost to get near it, and a book that puts the two in one column shows a smaller number than the truth in about four years.** **The same morning the fen entered the day and not the figure for the shelf well four hundred miles off — the fourth hour on the first of next month, a Saturday, not in this book — and entered that the count of that standing is three and that three is a practice and not a habit.** The door is read out whole at the tenth hour, **the seventh column goes to THIRTY-FIVE, the next reading day in this month is the thirtieth, and the door is read on the first and the thirtieth of a month and has been for four years.** The standing rule is read out in full, four lines, before the door is closed, and the fen entered that the count of the uses is one and **that it goes to two on the thirteenth and not before**, and that the third line does not say in a yard and this body read it as though it did for two years, and that the rule says nothing whatever about what comes after the stop. Panel: none. Notes: the **forty-seventh**, dated **the first**, **carrying the standing sentence.**
- **179 — *A Licensed Use*** — days 302–303, the second and the third, a Sunday and a Monday. On the Sunday the holding does almost nothing and the fen entered that as a fact and not as a virtue, and two carts are loaded for Thursday, and the reader of this body is not in the room where the seed side's list is checked and did not write and ask. **On the third, a form comes up the fen road on the ordinary post from the sub-office at Fennmere, four lines, correct, in a clerk's square hand**, and it says **a migration along a road is a licensed use of that road, with a rate and a class; the rate is fourpence the hundredweight of goods carried and the class of migration is assigned by the office and not by the party; a body of households on one head is a party to a licensed use in the same way a holder of a well is a party, and a party has the standing of a party and not of a holder and not of an office; and the fourth line states that no part of it is a finding against any person and that nothing in it states that any person has done anything, and was read out twice.** The person who keeps the seed house says what it is standing up before anybody reads it, and **says the sentence that the third line is the same sentence as the fourth line of a return a month ago about a party supplying water at no price, and that a body which has now been a party twice in a month has stopped being a name and started being a category.** **THE FRAME, SAID STANDING UP, BEFORE ANYBODY COULD TURN IT INTO A FIGHT: AN OFFICE IS NOT A VILLAIN — said once in this book's history in a room of about four people, said now in a yard of about eleven, and it is going to keep needing to be said; and the reason given is that nobody in that office has lied to this holding or obstructed a document or delayed anything, that the office is not a person, and that being angry inside a structure is how a body ends up doing nothing in a yard and calling it patience.** A woman of about sixty with four pails asks **which road**, once, and the steward answers it in one sentence out loud before the reader of this body can, and the fen entered that the reader of this body did not take it off her. **The arithmetic: the rate of fourpence the hundredweight is derived out of two figures this holding took itself, twice, and the two agree — one thousand and forty-five hundredweight at sixteen pence is sixteen thousand seven hundred and twenty pence, being one thousand three hundred and ninety-three shillings and four pence, being sixty-nine pounds thirteen shillings and fourpence, against fifty-two pounds five shillings paid, a difference of seventeen pounds eight shillings and fourpence, being four thousand one hundred and eighty pence; and one thousand and forty-five hundredweight at a shilling is twelve thousand five hundred and forty pence, and the two come to sixteen thousand seven hundred and twenty; and four thousand one hundred and eighty pence on one thousand and forty-five hundredweight is four pence. And the woman who does the sums says she is not glad about it, and gives the reason: a figure she could check came down a road in somebody else's hand and told her nothing she did not already have.** **The count of the things this holding has been told no by this season stands at six and nothing in that yard was a no and nothing was a yes, and a thing a form says that this body does not want is not a refusal and must not be counted as one.** The fen entered that it cannot name a single household in a migration and will not try, **and that the difference between not knowing and refusing has been the whole argument of this book for a year and is not going to be collapsed a second time because a piece of paper came in the post.** Panel: none. Notes: none.
- **180 — *Soldiers On The Fen Road*** — days 304–305, the fourth and the fifth, a Tuesday and a Wednesday. **An inspection comes up the fen road in the rain at about the fourth hour and stops at the gate and there are soldiers on the road and they are soldiers and nobody in this yard asked them to be anything else.** They stood in the rain for about an hour and a half, two of them came to the gate posts and did not come past them, **the engineer of record was in the low ground and did not come up and was not called, the licensed man's case was in the cart shed where it has been since the ninth of last month, and nothing on this holding was open that was not open every day of a year.** The officer was let in without a question and without a delay. He asked two questions, both about the yard and neither about a person: how many households are on this head, **sixteen**, and whether there is a licence, **there is not and there has not been for two years**, and both answers were written down in the ordinary way and no third question was asked. **Nobody obstructed anybody, nothing was delayed, no door was shut in an officer's face, and the fen entered all three separately and entered that they are the correct conduct of this holding and not a favour to anybody.** The officer's form, read out in the yard: an inspection in the ordinary course at the hour and order in the schedule attached; **a body of households on one head that uses a road is a party to the use of it and is to be returned under the class assigned to it, and the class assigned is migration and was assigned by the office and not by the party**; **no obstruction, delay or refusal is to be offered, an inspection may not be refused, and a body that has a question about a form is to put the question in the form and not in a yard**; and the fourth line, that a party entered under a class is not a holder of anything by being entered under a class. **The reader of this body says the reason out loud before he fills the box in: a body that does not put itself in a class is a body that somebody else puts in it; a class is a thing an office can use against a body in a room, in a letter, in a year, when there is nobody in the room who was in the yard; and he says he has watched that done to a body of four houses on the other side of the district and is not going to be put in one by a man with a book who has done nothing wrong.** He says the third line is correct and that the office is telling this holding in writing that a yard is not a form, and that it has not got a form, and that it is not going to be embarrassed about that in front of the man who wrote the line. **THE CHAPTER: at the reader of this body's request and not the officer's, the standing figures of this holding are read out whole and unsoftened in a yard of about twenty-five people — the compost line at eighty pounds, the leak, the beat with its column open and the column not opened, the seventh column at thirty-five, the standing rule, the use log at fifteen lines, the rootmark and the four accounts of the arm, the form on a bench, and the well on a shelf at five feet against nine feet for the sixteenth of a month six months back — and the fen entered that this is the first time in eleven years that this holding's figures have been read out loud to a person who came to find out whether they were true, and entered that this body asked for it, and entered why: a body that keeps a book anybody may read ought to be the first to hand it over, and the alternative is a body which only reads its book when there is nobody in the yard who can check it.** The four things **not** entered — the form on the bench, the column of the beat, the fourth cart, the word a man of about thirty-five has refused for nineteen years — each with the same reason, **and the officer did not ask for any of them, and the fen entered that he did not ask and entered the reason: he is not the office and this is not an examination of a person.** The officer wrote one line and read it back before closing the case. **THE CLERK'S OBSERVATION, the one available in this batch, entered on his own motion and unasked and not explained by anybody: THE FIGURES OF THIS HOLDING WERE READ OUT LOUD, IN A YARD, TO A MAN WHO CAME TO FIND OUT WHETHER THEY WERE TRUE.** On the Wednesday the licensed man's standing is said out loud — **a man on the water record can be on this road and look at a shaft and say a thing about water and cannot lay a graft and cannot go near a living root** — and the second half, that there were four soldiers on that road yesterday at the fourth hour and one of them was wet through to the shirt, and that in nineteen years he has never had a reason to be glad a road had soldiers on it and is not going to pretend to have one now, **and the fen entered that he was not on this holding's time that day and did not charge for it.** The officers wrote the time of their leaving on the back of their own sheet at the gate. Panel: none. Notes: none.
- **181 — *The Seed Side Goes On The Road*** — days 306–307, the sixth and the seventh, a Thursday and a Friday. **The seed side of this holding goes onto a road and it is a working movement and not a departure.** Two carts out on the Thursday at about the seventh hour and back on the Friday at about the eighth hour; the count of carts, sacks, and the households' seed that went and stayed was made in a yard on the Friday at about the fourth hour **by four households in turns, the way a gate count is made**, and the fen entered that **this is the only count of its kind in this book's history and that it is a count of carts and not a decision**, and the man of about fifty's initial went in against nothing, the count of that request being forty this season. Nine sacks on the first cart and seven on the second and the two that did not go were the two nearest the wall, opened on the boards in the rain and looked into and closed again; **the engineer of record carried eleven sacks and got into the cart bed and put them where she pointed and was not asked to and was not thanked and was not needed, and the fen entered all four.** One exchange on the Thursday, entered entire: the seedwright said **wet** about the fourth sack, the engineer of record gave a reason and did not argue, **that exchange is the whole of what two of them said to each other on the Thursday.** **The four costs a road costs, on four lines and not added together: the cart, which is hired and is not ours; the driver's day; the two days off four households; and the four hundred yards a person has to walk to find out where a cart has got to, which is not on any line in any book and costs the most.** **Every one of those was paid by the seed trust at its own cost, out of a fund this holding has never seen a figure from: this holding did not pay any of it, could not pay any of it, and is not going to be thanked for the road, and the fen entered the last of those on the page because in about four years somebody in that yard is going to describe a woman taking a cart and forty households' seed onto a road and somebody is going to say this holding helped, and the truth is that this holding could not have bought the cart.** **The reason, in about five sentences, standing up, and it is not the one anybody expected: a seed route is a licensed thing on a road that has been declared a licensed use; a trust with seed on a licensed road is a party to a licensed use; a body that has been a party twice in a month is a category, and she has watched what a category is for because she is the one who reads the returns of the people who make categories; and she is not going to be a category with forty households' seed in a cart on a road with soldiers standing at the gate of the next place, and would rather be slow.** **The reader of this body did four things, entered separately: he did not argue with it, did not ask her to wait, did not offer a way of doing it that is better, and did not thank her — and the fen entered that he did none of the four and did not consider doing any of the four.** Then he said his whole position once and she did not answer it: **a seed on a licensed road can be carried by a body that has refused to be a party; he has looked for one for eleven years, hardest in the four places where somebody has already been punished for being a party; there is not one in this district that he has ever heard of; and he is not going to pretend that this holding is one, because it has no charter and sixteen households on one head and put itself in a class in its own hand on Tuesday in front of a man with a book, and would do it again in the morning, and that is not a foundation anybody should be asked to build a seed route on.** **The separation, entered as a working arrangement and not a quarrel: the seed side takes the seed, the route and the record of the route, which is the trust's and is kept by the trust; this holding keeps the water, the well, the leak, the beat, the door and the four lines; neither of the two said the word and the fen entered that the word was not said and did not enter the word; and the count of things this holding used to do with a person is now a count of things it does not do, and the two lists are on facing pages and are not to be read as a grievance by anybody in four years.** Her question, **what happens to the gate count of the four households on a seed side that is not on this head** — they keep it, it was always their count, and this holding has been reading a copy of it for eleven years and the copy is not the count — and her second half, that a record which cannot go where the work goes is not a record but a place where somebody kept a copy. And one household of the four, told on the Friday that the two sacks that had not gone were the two it wanted out of its own ground before the sixteenth, asked whether the seed side was coming back for them, **and was told that a seed side is on a road and not on a calendar, and that a household should not be given a day by a body that has not got one.** The seedwright came up the road on the Friday evening and slept on the bench in the cart shed. Panel: none. Notes: none.
- **182 — *In Front Of People*** — day 308, the eighth, a Saturday. **At about the eleventh hour, in a full yard of about twenty-five people, the reader of this body said the thing he has been carrying since a room with the doors shut, standing up, and nobody made him.** He had told one person on the Thursday night that he was going to say a thing in that yard on the Saturday at the eleventh hour and had not said what it was, **and she asked him nothing about it, and the fen entered that a man who says out loud on a Thursday night that he is going to say something on a Saturday has given the yard the day and the hour and kept the thing.** The ordinary business of that hour carried on around it — two of them arguing about a gate post and the man of about fifty being told his cart shed roof was not going to last the month — and **the fen entered that none of it stopped, and that this is the first time in this book's history that something has been said out loud in a yard of about twenty-five people and the ordinary business of a Saturday carried on around it at the same time, and that he asked for the eleventh hour and not the fourth hour because at the fourth hour the yard is empty and at the eleventh hour it is full, and a thing said to nine people is a thing said to a room and a thing said to twenty-five is a thing said to a yard.** **He gives the date of the room, then the hour, then the number of people in it, in that order: the twenty-fourth of a month two months back, about the fourth hour of the afternoon, a room with the doors shut, four of them in it and one of them the clerk, who confirmed the hour out loud in the ordinary voice from his own hand before the reader of this body got to the fourth sentence.** **The thing, in about six sentences: he said it to one person and would not write it down; the reason he gave at the time is that he could not defend it and had not got a second reading, and that reason was a good reason then and is not a good reason today; the date it named fell on a Sunday, the eleventh of last month, on which nothing happened and there was nothing to be honest about; the man who did it is the one who has to say that, and he said it in front of him and not behind his back; and he is saying it in front of nine hundred people this morning, and there is nothing on this page to be honest about, and he would like that entered. THE FEN ENTERED THAT THE NUMBER IS NOT A COUNT AND DID NOT CORRECT IT, and gave the reason: a man making an apology is entitled to one wrong number about how many people are hearing him, and it is the only number in this book this holding is not going to check.** Then four more: **he did it to one person and not to a body; a thing that is in one room and not in a book is a thing that only one person can be asked to carry, and I made a person into a book, and I would not have done that to a stranger; and he wants it entered that he would not have done it to a stranger, and that the fact that he would not have done it to a stranger is not a defence and is the reason it was worse; and he asked for no forgiveness and said he would not be thanked and said it is not that kind of day.** **The clerk entered it and read it back in the ordinary voice and did not improve it. NOBODY THANKED HIM, nobody said a word, and the fen entered that nobody treated it as a thing between two people, and entered the reason: about twenty-five people watched a man say a thing out loud in daylight, and a thing said out loud in a yard is a fact about a yard and not a thing two people have.** She answered in one sentence, in the ordinary voice, at the wall side, to about twenty-five people and not to him, **and it was not forgiveness and it was not a refusal: I am not going to say that makes it right and I am not going to say it does not, and I am going to go on the road on Monday, and the reason I am going is on a page with my name at the top of it and not on this one, and I would like it entered that I said the reason is on another page.** She then sat on the bench in the cart shed and worked on the trust's list until the yard emptied, and nobody sat down with her. **She was not thanked, not forgiven on the page, and forgave nothing on the page, and the fen entered that nobody treated the answer as forgiveness and entered that nobody treated it as a refusal either and is not going to improve on the answer or find out which of the two it was.** Then, before the figures, **the reader of this body said he was not going to say that again this month, and gave the reason: a thing said once in a yard is a thing a man did and a thing said twice is a standing this holding would have to keep, and he has watched this holding get a standing it did not ask for out of a thing a man said once in a room.** And at the fourth hour the question went into a yard of about nine and nobody took it, and the fen entered that **the count of the times this body has asked it is three and answered never, and the count of the times it has been available to a room in this holding and not taken is six, three of which are this question, and both counts stand and neither has been added to the other.** Panel: none. Notes: none.
- **183 — *The Name Becomes A Person*** — days 309–310, the ninth and the tenth, a Sunday and a Monday. **On the ninth a letter comes up the fen road on the ordinary post, four lines, correct, on the party's own paper and not an office's**: the supply at no price made into the low quarter between the second and the seventh of last month **has not been resumed and the reason is not given and no reason is asked for**; **the party will be in the district on the tenth and a person will come to this holding on foot and will stand in whatever yard this holding keeps its book in**; that person is the person who signs these returns and his name is at the foot of the sheet, **and a party is a party and is not an office and has no standing over anybody**; and the fourth line, that it asks for no form, no certificate, no answer and no water. **It is a letter and it is not a return, and the fen entered the difference: a return is a thing an office keeps and a letter is a thing a person sends, and this holding has had four returns in two months and has never had a letter.** **On the tenth he comes up the fen road on foot at about the second hour of the afternoon, unannounced, and is announced before he speaks**, standing up in a yard of about eleven by the person who keeps the seed house, who says he is not of this body, is not on anything and is not a representative of anything, and that she is not going to start doing it differently for him. **He gives his name and his position in six sentences in a form that could be read back: MY NAME IS TARIN CALLOW. I AM THE PERSON WHO SIGNS THE RETURNS OF THE PALE ORCHARD. I HELD A POST IN THE DISTRICT WATER OFFICE AT FENNHERE FOR SIX YEARS AND I DO NOT HOLD ONE NOW AND I HAVE NOT HELD ONE FOR ELEVEN. I HAVE NO LICENCE AND NO CHARTER AND NO PAPER EXCEPT THAT ONE. I AM NOT A REPRESENTATIVE OF ANYTHING AND I AM NOT ANYBODY'S GUIDE.** **He is correct in every particular and the fen entered that on the day, and entered that a man being correct in every particular in a yard of about eleven people is a harder thing to write down than a man being wrong, and that this holding has far more practice at the second.** **And in the first ten minutes he corrects this holding, against the party and not against this holding, and says he does it in the first ten minutes so that nobody can think for the rest of the afternoon that he came to be liked: WE WERE NOT GIVING IT. THE SUPPLY WAS ALREADY PAID FOR. There is a line in our own accounts that says what it cost and I have not got it with me and I am not going to read you a figure I cannot show you. That is the correction. It is not a smaller thing than the one you have written down. It is a different thing, and it is worse, because what you have written down is that we did something for them and what is true is that we moved our own water through their quarter.** The fen entered that this is the first time in this book's history that a person has corrected this holding against his own interest, and that it does not settle anything. **Then the true thing, and it is true, and this holding has spent a year arguing against guessing and cannot answer one word of it: a controlled system does not fail the way an uncontrolled one fails; an uncontrolled system leaves people to find out, and the four wells in that lane went off on four different days in a month and every household worked out for itself what to do about it, some of them finding out before anybody else and some of them last, and a quarter of four hundred and forty-one people spent a month doing arithmetic about water with no instrument; and a system with a schedule and a rate and a class of use and a form does not do that, and a quarter that has a form on it is a quarter in which nobody has to guess where the water went, and you can go and read it, so can he, so can the man with the cart, and a page that is dated is worth more to a person with a fever than a well-informed neighbour.** **Then the method, not softened, at the same voice and the same speed, and he says he is not going to put it after the true thing to get the true thing said first: the schedule is made, the class is chosen, the people in the class are counted; a count is made of who is in a quarter so that the quarter can be supplied; and a quarter supplied on a schedule for a season has a supply that belongs to the supply and not to the people in it, and when the schedule changes the people in it are not a people any more, they are a class that has moved or a class that has not; and he knows what that sentence sounds like on the other side of a form, and has written it in a letter this week and had it not typed, and wants that on the page in his words and not in a summary.** Two of the eleven went out of the yard and nobody followed them, **and the fen entered why: a man who has just said a true thing and then said the thing the true thing is built on does not get followed out of a yard by a body that has learned something this month about letting people be finished.** **The demand, which is correct and is a document: that this holding will state, in its own book and on a date, the number of people in the low quarter, where they are, and how many of them there are. The reader of this body cannot refuse it and cannot accept it and said so out loud in one sentence, and the fen entered both separately: it cannot refuse, because a body that refuses a question in the open has said the question; it cannot accept, because this holding does not know the answer and has said so for two months in a room and in a yard and on a bench where a form is sitting unanswered; and the difference between those two is the whole argument of this volume in one line.** Then one question asked in the open with the reason first — **what the party is for** — and the checkable half in about four sentences: **a party is a set of people who have agreed to be answerable for one another's water; there were four of them at the start on a road out of a district where nobody was going to answer for anything, and they wrote it down and gave each other the right to be asked by a household that had nothing, and that was the whole of the invention and it is not clever and should not have taken a party to think of.** And the half he will not give: **why they are organized that way and what they intend to be in five years, and the reason is that it is not checkable, and that anything else would be four months of words that sound like a body admitting what it does not know and are not. The fen entered that the count of that refusal is one and entered that the count of one is a count of this morning and not a count of a man, and that it is not going to be carried forward as a standing.** He was not thanked, nobody was forgiven anything, and the reader of this body did not shake a hand and did not offer a chair. Panel: none. Notes: none.
- **184 — *What It Did, And What It Said*** — days 311–312, the eleventh and the twelfth, a Tuesday and a Wednesday. **On the eleventh a return comes up the fen road on the ordinary post, four lines, correct, the party's own return, reporting a death in its own operation in its own words in the ordinary course**: on the eighth, moving water into a quarter in which wells are off, **one man died, he was thirty-one, he is named in the return of the district and not in this one**; the manner was a fall of about nine feet onto stone while a pipe was being laid, and the party paid for the stone to be taken up and the ground made good **and did not pay for the man and could not**; the party reports the death against itself and does not report a cause and says in that line that it is not able to give a cause anybody could check; and the fourth line, that a party that reports a death in its own operation is a party and is entered as one. **Nobody lies in it and nothing is softened.** **The arithmetic, done in a yard because nobody would go back into the room: what this holding can check is three figures and a day, and she says she is not going to count them out loud because it would take forty minutes and make a spectacle of a dead man; what this holding cannot check is whether the schedule put him there, whether the count put him there, whether the rate and the class and the return made a man of thirty-one be on that road on that day with a pipe in his hands instead of somewhere else with a wage; the two are on one line and are not added together; and she says the sentence again anyway — a figure which is the sum of two things one of which was never measured is not a figure, and it is not going into this book, and this is the sixth time in two months that this holding has been beaten home by somebody else's arithmetic, and the count of six is entered and is not going to stop being entered.** **The death is on a page. There is no body in a room in this chapter and there was not one in a yard either, and the fen entered that the reader of this body did not ask a single question about the man and entered why: a man is thirty-one and dead on a piece of paper and the only thing this holding could offer him is a question.** **And in the evening, with the bay doors shut and about eleven people in the room, a woman of a household in the low acre came up the fen road in wet boots and sat down and did not take them off and nobody asked her to, and the trust's ledger was shut on the table in front of the seedwright of the trust and was not opened while that woman was talking, and the fen entered that it was shut by the person whose it is.** **Six sentences, entered entire, and she says both halves of what she came to say in the same breath and is not asked to choose: she is not going to talk about the man, she did not know him, and anybody who thinks she came up nine miles to be sorry has misunderstood the whole of the evening; she is here because the party is not wrong about the schedule and is wrong about the count, and she has worked that out on her own feet; A COUNT OF WHO IS IN A QUARTER IS A COUNT OF WHO CAN BE REACHED — you do not count a quarter so that you know the quarter, you count it so that you can get to it; my household was not in it and my household's man is one of the people it did not reach, and he is in the low acre and not in the low quarter, and he has been on the same road as those men every day of his life and is not on any page, and nobody counted him because there was no reason to count him; so both halves of that are true and she is not going to be asked to pick one, and the party is not a bad office, it is four people who did exactly what she has just described, correctly, in the order they had to do it in, got it wrong, knew they were getting it wrong, and wrote it on a page; and she is not here to tell anybody what to do about it and wants that said in her house's name and not her own, and is not going to name a person in that yard in the low acre, because she said on the twenty-second of last month that she did not want a body that is not a quarter to come, and has not changed her mind, and there is a difference between telling a thing in a book and bringing a body, and she is the one who has to carry her own door.** **And then the sentence the volume needs, said once, out loud, in the open, by a person who is not the accused and not the accuser: ANNEKE ROOS said, standing up, in the ordinary voice, THE ACCUSATION AND THE METHOD ARE TWO THINGS, AND WE HAVE BEEN ARGUING WITH ONE OF THEM FOR A MONTH.** Then the reader of this body's second half, which is the half he is going to be blamed for: **a method that counts who can be reached is a method that will reach the people it did not count the next time, and he says it out loud in this room because everybody in this room has already said it out loud to themselves, and there is no point in the ninth hour of the evening finding out who has been thinking it; and a thing said to yourself is a thing this body has a habit of, and it is not a good habit, and he has been in four rooms in two months where everybody knew what was happening and nobody had it written down, and he was in one of the four, and that is how a page about a man who is thirty-one and dead gets written and nobody ever has to answer for it.** Then the woman of the low acre asked, in the open, what this holding is going to do about it, and he gave the only honest sentence available: **it has no standing, no charter, no form on which to put that number, and no second reading of anything, and the count of the things this body does not know is four and is not going to be five this month because a man said a hard thing in a room; so it is going to write it down with the hour on it and read it back out loud in front of the people it is about; and those two things are worth very little, and a page is not a supply and a reading-out is not an answer, and the woman who asked is owed more than a page and this body cannot give her more than a page.** Nobody thanked anybody. Panel: none. Notes: none.
- **185 — *What Comes After The Stop*** — day 313, the thirteenth, a Thursday. **The second and last use of the standing rule in this volume, and the only draw in the batch, and the two are the same event.** At about the fourth hour in the low ground four hundred yards below the north gate, at the boards over a shaft that came off on the twenty-first of a month three months back and was put back together on the twentieth of last month, a draw is begun with a licensed man holding the book and the hour on it, and the reader of this body at the top of the slope with about nine people, **and he did not go down and gave the reason out loud before anybody asked him: a body that stands over a crew is a body the crew talks to, and this holding has four people on a floor and no custom for it, and he had been told that on the nineteenth of last month and has not improved on it since.** The man of the north row with the cough went down the ladder with the shovel three times in forty minutes, was told out loud that he was not to be asked to do it again, said nothing about that and did it anyway, **and the fen entered that a man who is told he is not to be asked and does the thing is not a man who has agreed to it and is not a man who has refused it either, and that the fen does not have a word for that and is not going to find one this month.** The return was read at the fifth hour of the evening of the twelfth and **the first line was established before the draw was begun, which the fen entered is the first time in the four years of that rule that it has been written down first, and the first line is kept and is kept first.** Nobody obstructed anybody, nothing was delayed, no door was shut in a face, entered separately as the correct conduct of a Thursday morning. Forty minutes of ordinary work, the level taken before and after and two numbers written side by side and **no difference written.** **The rule is read out in full, four lines, in the yard at about the fourth hour before any of that, and it is read out for the third time this month, and the count of the readings and the count of the uses are two counts and are not the same count, and the fen entered that a rule read out on the morning it is used is not a rule that is being kept, and that the mornings it is not used are the ones this body would have to give up first.** The draw is begun at about the half hour past four and is entered as the second in this volume and the last. **At four feet the licensed man called the stop out loud, in the ordinary voice, from the head of the shaft, to about four people on the boards; two of the men stopped before he had finished the sentence, which the fen entered, because a stop is a stop and the half second in which it is not yet obeyed is not a failure of the rule and is not going to be written down as one; and the stop was entered by whoever held the book, which was that licensed man, in his own hand at the head of the shaft with the hour on it, and the clerk entered the same thing in this holding's book and read it back down the slope so that the four people on the boards heard it entered.** **The rule is satisfied. Every one of the four lines is satisfied. Nobody has broken anything and nobody has a defence, and the fen entered that this is the second use in this volume and the last and that the count of the uses is now two and is not going to be three. AND THE DRAW GOT NO LINE IN THE USE LOG AND THE CHAPTER SAYS SO ON THE PAGE: a line records a contact and never a reading, a draw gets no line, a hold gets no line, the log stands at fifteen lines, and the two contacts since the ninth of last month and the hold in between have got no line either, and the reason the log stands at fifteen is that this holding cannot write down what it did on the boards in a line anybody could check.** **Then the thing no rule in this book has ever been about, said out loud on the slope with about nine people standing in it: nine people came up that slope at about the fourth hour to see whether a thing could be got through to a road, and their names are in the book and he is not going to read them out and is not going to count them, because a count would be a thing they did not come for; THEY DID NOT GET IT — the draw stopped at four feet, the four lines were satisfied, the book says what happened, and about four hundred yards of a road on the other side of that ground did not open, and every part of it was correct; and then THE SENTENCE: WE HAVE WRITTEN A RULE ABOUT WAITING AND WE HAVE CALLED IT A RULE ABOUT CARE. ANY BODY CAN OBEY IT PERFECTLY FOR EVER AND NEVER DO A THING AGAIN AND IT WILL LOOK EXACTLY LIKE A BODY THAT IS BEING CAREFUL. And that it is the second time this book has said that sentence, the first having been in a room in this building with about eleven people in it, the day not given, the clerk entering that he did not say the day.** The nine are entered by what they came for and not by name: a woman of a household in the low acre whose household's man was told on the Sunday; a driver on the road the carts went onto, who came up with them on the Friday; a second driver's household; a woman of a household in the low quarter; and the man of about fifty's household, which is at the top of that slope most days of its life. **One of them has a fever, he is not one of the nine, he is a household in the low acre, and he is not the reason the nine came up; and one of the nine has lost a day of carrying that is not going to be repaid in anything, in money or in seed or in a week, and the reader of this body looked for about four minutes for a way to repay it and there is not one, and the fen entered that he looked.** **The arithmetic, said out loud on the slope, derived twice, the two agree, and the examiner took the second through a third with her finger on the page after saying out loud that she keeps time and does not round: one day of a licensed man this month, the thirteenth, at one pound a day, is twenty shillings, which is one pound; and four pounds and fivepence is eighty-five shillings, less twenty, is sixty-five shillings, which is three pounds and five shillings, and in pence a thousand and twenty less two hundred and forty is seven hundred and eighty pence, which is sixty-five shillings, which is three pounds and five shillings. And she says the next part faster this month and does not split it: A COST THIS HOLDING CAN COMPUTE IS A COST IT CAN HAND TO AN OFFICE — said in a room a month ago and on the first of this month and a third time now, and she is not going to be glad about it and is not going to be sorry about it, and nobody is going to thank her for it.** Then **the cost, in the engineer's own hand, in a line of its own, at the ninth hour in the cart shed with the door open and the clerk standing in it: THE MARK IS HOT. THE HAND FROM THE WRIST DOWN WAS NO USE FOR ABOUT A DAY. A HEADACHE BEHIND THE EYES FROM ABOUT THE SEVENTH HOUR LAST NIGHT UNTIL ABOUT THE FOURTH HOUR THIS MORNING, WHICH IS NOT THE MARK AND IS NOT A MARK AND IS NOT TO BE ENTERED AS ONE. FOUR FEET. And on the same line, in the clerk's hand: the four accounts of the arm stand where they stood, THIS IS NOT A FIFTH ACCOUNT AND IT DOES NOT RECONCILE ANY OF THEM.** Panel: none. Notes: none.
- **186 — *What The Road Is For*** — days 314–315, the fourteenth and the fifteenth, a Friday and a Saturday. **The man of the north row with the cough walks out to the low quarter at about the fifth hour on the Friday and back at about the seventh hour of the evening and comes into a yard of about nine without sitting down and has two things and asks nobody anything about either**, and the fen entered that he did not and that nobody tried to make him. **The first, in four sentences in the plainest words he has: THE PAILS HAVE GOT LONGER AND THE WALKS HAVE NOT — whatever is going on, the water is further off than it was and the distance a person walks to it is exactly what it was; the quarter is not being supplied on a charter, it is being supplied on a class, and a charter is a thing somebody can point at and a class is a thing somebody has been put in, and the four that stopped saying it and the one that started asking who is paying are all still in those houses and nobody out there has been told a word about any of this week;
