THE BOTTLENECK SENTENCE: a running count of five and an itemised list of five that share three members and cannot both be right. THE COUNT OF QUESTIONS: nine on the page against a guardrail that says no higher than eight. `favour` and `licence`: British forms the closed volumes also carry, left standing on purpose. The wall's source: Ch 168, in a closed volume, with the same wrong list still standing in `workspace/volume-05/batch-0004/PROMPT.md:85`.** All six are set out in full in `state/open-threads.md` under *State after the VOLUME 05 CLOSE*, items 44 to 48, and in `reviews/volume-05.findings.md` at §1, §10, §11 and §12.

# VOLUME 06 — BATCH 0001, CHAPTERS 246–255, DAYS 451–461, MOVEMENT 1: WHAT A FIGURE IS FOR

**`workspace/volume-06/batch-0001/`. Ten chapters, 29,687 words, range 2,614–3,119, mean 2968.7, all ten inside the 2,600–3,120 band, no chapter declared outside it, band not widened. `Entered ...` block counts 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 0, and Ch 255 carries the batch climax and takes none.**

**246 — *One Hundred And Forty*** (day 451, the first of the fourth month, a Tuesday)
The compost line falls and is entered whole: PAID, one hundred pounds, fifteen not discharged in fifteen, and the word before the figure is PAID for the second month running and a word is a thing a person picks. The standing for the first is read at nine hundred yards off in about twenty-five people by the person who keeps the seed house and read back by the clerk, and **the reader of this body is five days east and is not in that yard and did not ask for the sheet.** Seventh column forty-four, six other columns blank four years and nobody asked, day-minus rules 385 and 432, the leak named and not measured with no fourth reason invented, the beat not logged and its column not opened, the use log at fifteen with what got no line, the standing rule named once and not used, both pattern-holder counts and both new counts, the four accounts of the arm, the day-and-not-the-figure at three. **The eighteenth ninth-day return is ONE HUNDRED AND FORTY HUNDREDWEIGHT** against about ninety at the last four, the eighth name-pair in eleven years, and **Harlan Vetch leaves the second rule blank for the fourth time and writes nothing in it and gives his reason out loud and is not thanked, and the count of the returns at which he has not written in it is two.** The four launders measure **nine hundred, six hundred, five hundred and seventy-five and twenty-five**, the four total two thousand a hundred and twenty-five, the node's own delivered figure is about two thousand one hundred, the four bodies count about two thousand one hundred, **and the difference is nothing, and the twenty-five is inside somebody's about.** He walks to the saw-cut and does not measure it and does not put a rule against it, walks past the ring of bare ground four times, and says the two things he came east to say with the reason first. A man of about seventy-one of Silling says one thing about a ditch that came up on the twenty-eighth of a month three months back. The horse cost two shillings and eightpence and is a line of its own.

**247 — *Up Out Of The Ground*** (day 452, a Wednesday)
A yard at Errow Reach in about nineteen people and the reader of this body tells four bodies of households **that the water is coming up out of the ground under the Four Elbows**, with the reason first, and the reason is that a launder that carries nothing has got to be fed from somewhere. **It is wrong, and the wrong part is the reason and not the sentence, and he says so himself before anybody says it to him. THE COUNT OF THINGS HE HAS SAID OUT LOUD IN A YARD AND GOT WRONG IS THREE AND IT IS FOUR, AND THE WORD ON THE FOURTH IS *up*, and it was available to him on the twentieth of a month two months back and he did not take it.** The woman who keeps the four books reads the covenant's fifth line in about nine seconds and it is a true line and it is not a true line any more. **The man of the north row with the cough, at the eighteenth of his yards and not asked, says that the kerb stone moved under his knee on the eleventh of the first month and that the cut is about two feet because of him and not because of the saw, and says it in about nineteen people and not to one man, and is not thanked and says not to be.** The licensed man says that this node is about nine miles inside a district that is not the Tarrow district and that he has known it for eleven years. The four bodies put the auger down in four places they chose and the ground is wet at about two feet in all four, and he is in the second hole and cannot tell whether the water is coming up or going down, and **does not put the rule on the third launder, and gives the reason: two figures that agree with a sentence is a man and his own opinion holding hands.**

**248 — *Eleven Seconds*** (day 453, a Thursday)
One sheet on the ordinary post, on the embossed paper of **the Office of the Continuity, for the maintenance of the Crown Engine.** Six numbered lines and two at the foot, read out whole and entered whole. Zone four. A maintenance operation on the thirtieth of a month two months back at about the fourth hour of the afternoon and again at about the tenth hour of the evening. **The figures as eleven seconds and six hours and eleven seconds, and the office holds the same figures and does not propose to alter them.** The maintained season, a term of that office and not of the district, and **the office does not state on paper what the operation consists of on the ground.** Compensation at one shilling and twopence the hundredweight for one season, applications answered in the order received. **The maintenance will be reviewed before the next window.** At the foot, over an acknowledgment line for a person to sign, **CHANCELLOR IONA VEY**, and the line names **MAREK VALE, ENGINEER OF RECORD.** The reader of this body says the hard part first, with the reason first: **the sheet is correct, and it does not lie, and the office holds the fen's two figures to the second, and he is not going to accuse anybody of having a stopwatch on a sill, because he has nothing to stand on and because the document is the evidence and what he feels about the document is not a second document.** The woman who keeps the four books works out that **none of the four bodies of households can claim a hundredweight of the best rate they have ever been offered, because the flow is up and a claim is a claim about a flow a body counted and then did not get** — and the count of times a book-keeper has said a thing in a yard he had not thought of moves to five, and he counts it in a book and not in that yard. The licensed man says that zone four is a list and that an acknowledgment line is a claim. **The line is not signed and is not refused out loud and the reason is not entered.** The word *window* is the only word in the six lines that is not a word a person would use about a river.

**249 — *The Fifth Line Is False*** (day 454, a Friday)
The fourth line of the rota comes round and there is nobody at the headland above Marden and the fields wait, and the number is **eleven**, and all eleven of them are the fourth line, and the fourth line has now come round on eleven mornings since the rota came into force and has been empty on eleven of them. The covenant is read out again, all six lines, at the asking of Renn Ashby, who is given a copy. **The woman who keeps the four books says the second thing of her week: a sheet that says a figure cannot change is a sheet that has to be torn up, she has torn up two in eleven years and mended none, and neither was torn up because a figure was wrong, and she wants it entered in about nineteen people and not in a room that she said it.** **The four bodies of households ask whether they are to answer the sheet and the reader of this body says no unasked and gives the reason first: a claim is not a way of getting money, it is a way of being on a list, and the first number an office writes down about a body of households is the number that office will use about it for twenty years, and underneath that a claim is a way of being counted and four bodies of households have not been counted by anybody for eleven years and he does not know what the counting would be for, and a man who says no to a counting on the grounds that he does not know the reason for it has a preference and not a care.** The man of about fifty says the thing about the rota's tenth column, that it is a number about us and not about them. **Then Renn Ashby asks, in about nine words, whether anybody has asked Marden, and the answer is nobody, and the fen enters that this is the second thing the reader of this body has got wrong in three days, and that the count of things said out loud in a yard and got wrong is a count of one kind of wrong and does not move for the other kind, and that both kinds are on the same two pages and have not been added together.**

**250 — *Three Columns At One Hour*** (day 455, a Saturday)
A seedwright of a trust comes up the fen road with four hundredweight of boards and **Tova Reed is named**, and she reads four pages of a route book standing up in about nineteen people and **does not let one word of them be written down**, and both things are said out loud before anybody answers. Three pages are three places at one hour on the twenty-ninth of a month three months back — Cormel in the Salt March, where the ground thawed at about the second hour and a man of about seventy-four sowed at the fourth hour in a district with no rain for a season; the Long Fungal on the Thornwild edge, where eleven days of fruiting fell in one morning on a tree dead eleven years; and this holding's own node, where the flow went up for one morning and came back down, the figure given to her on the road and given back. The fourth page is **Ostermere Weir in the Auberron Basin**, nine weeks older, the best winter wheat in nine years, entered by the district's own officers. **The reader of this body checks the three connections himself against a nine-year-old chart and a four-year-old conduit book: no living path between any two of them, a lead maintenance conduit whose last entry is a flushing on the nineteenth of a month seven months back, eleven miles of salt flat and a salt pan, a chart line drawn by a man who is dead, and the fourth pair he could not do at all.** He says out loud that a thing which happens at the same hour in three places with no wire between any two of them is not weather, and that he has three things out of four and that the fourth is a person. **The person who keeps the seed house asks what they should call it and is told, and the two names are the maintained season and the kept weather, and the book on the middle table now carries both and the second came in on a cart with boards behind it.** The seedwright says a column of three figures is one person's afternoon and it is true, and **he concedes it once, in a yard, with the reason first, and writes it in his own book at about the seventh hour with the doors shut, and the count of rooms with the doors shut in this book's history is one and this is it.**

**251 — *The Frost With No Sky*** (day 456, a Sunday)
A frost at about the fifth hour of the morning, thin, in the furrows and the ruts and the shadow side of every post, and not on the road, holding until about the eighth hour, and **the sky clear at the fourth hour and clear at the fifth.** The person who keeps the seed house says so in about eleven people at the fifth hour because in about four hours it will be warm. **The frost book is thirty-one years old and has ONE line in it between the turn of the year and this morning, being the twelfth of the second month at the sixth hour with the sky clear.** The man of about fifty breaks the ice in the trough and says the thing that is his about a trough and it is nine inches of water and eleven beasts. **The man of the north row with the cough is in the yard at the nineteenth of his yards and is not asked and says nothing.** The reader of this body says the caution with the reason first: **a frost in the fourth month is a figure and not a cause, and he is not going to be the one who tells eleven people what put a skin on that ground, and he has said four things out loud in a yard and got wrong and knows the number and has known it every morning since Wednesday.** The third launder's figures for the last eleven days are read out of the woman's own book and read back in a second book and **the figure has not been the same twice since the first.** Four mats and eleven reed mats go over nine seed beds in an hour and ten minutes at the cost of eleven pounds and four shillings, a line of its own, and the reader of this body is not asked and does not offer. He walks the ring of bare ground at the shoulder, does not go down into it, and writes down why. **At about the seventh hour of the evening a woman comes up the fen road on foot from the north gate with a bag in each hand and asks for the seed house, and nobody had sent for her, and the reader of this body did not go to the road.**

**252 — *What She Knows*** (day 457, a Monday)
**Sera Quill is named.** She came up the road on foot with the same two bags, at her own cost, unasked, and the reader of this body gives the reason before he asks her for anything: he has not asked her a question in about four years and a yard is the only place he is going to ask her in, and about four of the eleven people are going to hear whatever she says. She looks at the sheet for about nine seconds and says she has seen the form of that before, that the form is not new and the zone is not new, and that a person who has seen the form of a thing twice can recognize it in about nine seconds — and then says the second thing herself, which is **that she is dangerous, and that if she is right the thing that took this holding's water is not an office, because an office has a door and a clerk and a stamp and will answer, and what she is describing has a shape and a schedule and instructions written by somebody who is not going to be in the room.** The reader of this body says four sentences with the reason first: **he is not going to ask her how many times she has seen it; she has a schedule in an earlier hand in her bag and he did not ask for it and did not look at it; and he is not going to trade his count of four for it.** **Lissa says the one thing, in the yard, and it is not tender: she is not going to say the woman is welcome and she is going to say she is going to be fed, and those are two different things, and she has been keeping them apart for about four years.** He does not answer her, in the yard or afterward. **The two of them work for about an hour and a half and Sera Quill defines an irregular branch as one whose flow has moved by more than a stated fraction across a stated number of readings in a stated time, all three of those numbers being in an instruction she has never seen, and they do not agree about whether an irregular branch is a damaged one or an unpredicted one, and neither will change and neither is asked to.** The reader of this body lays the third launder's eight readings beside a ditch that came up on the twenty-eighth and an operation on the thirtieth and a middle that came down a foot and one inch in eight mornings, and says **he has been doing the office's arithmetic for four years without being paid for it.** At about the seventh hour he writes his own name and his family name at the top of a clean page in his own book for the first time in five volumes, with the reason, and nobody is to be shown the page.

**253 — *Four Lines On A Piece Of Paper*** (days 458 and 459, a Tuesday and a Wednesday)
The fourth line of the rota came round on the eighth of this month and there was nobody at the headland above Marden and the fields waited, and the number is **twelve**. **At about the fourth hour of the morning of the eighth the hand of the reader of this body came back off the wrist down, over about nine seconds, and it is not a cure.** **The fifteenth ordinary reading of the well at Cauldron Reach is on the sixteenth of this month, which is eight days from this morning, and the day was entered and the figure was not, and the count of day-and-not-the-figure entries stands at three and is neither raised nor reduced.** Two pieces of paper are read out in about eleven people: **four lines in the hand of a seedwright of a trust, and three in the hand of an engineer.** The seedwright's: a word that is hers and that he does not get to choose or hear in advance; a second person in the room who is neither of them and is told before; he stops when she stops and not when he thinks she should or when he thinks she is being brave; **I am not his safety device, and if either of us is using me as one then either of us ends it and neither of us is asked why.** The engineer's: nineteen minutes at a time and not four times in a day; the bead on the person and not on the ground, and the cost told before; a second steward with a watch and a stop. **They differ in one place and it is not the numbers: hers has a line in it about a person and his does not, and the reason is that she has never once been the person who stops, and a woman who has never been the one who stops writes a line about a watch.** Both are to be used, **and when they contradict one another hers wins**, and that was entered out loud, and **he is not using anybody yet and is not going to go and make a thing to read so that he can be the man who tried.** The seedwright leaves and says the thing about her own road, **and tells him that his sister is on the western relay and is well and is not coming back for the wet, and that is the one chapter in this holding's book in which that name stands.** The letter to the Assembly went on the sixth and the answer came on the seventh, in one day, having said it has no officer who reads patterns. **Sabra Ollerton, of about fifty-two, inspector of the Rootwright Assembly for four districts, not on the roll for the district this node stands in, at her own cost, compares the Cormel column with the district's own ninth-day return in about forty minutes, and the two say the same thing to the hour. She says out loud that she is the wrong person for it, that her office is not the office on the sheet, and that the difference is embossed on the corner of the paper, and that nobody in nineteen years has ever asked her to corroborate a figure.** Nobody thanked anybody.

**254 — *Eleven People And Nine Acres*** (day 460, a Thursday)
**The nineteenth ninth-day return is ONE HUNDRED AND FIFTY-ONE HUNDREDWEIGHT, the ninth name-pair in eleven years, and the second rule is blank again and he says nothing, and the count of the returns at which he has not written in it is three.** The woman of about thirty-eight of Marden comes up onto the shoulder and says two things at the kerb in about nine people, and they are not a speech: **it has been standing against that gate since about the second of this month and she has walked past it every morning for about nine days and did not open it because it was not hers to open; and nine acres, and about eleven of us work it, and it came up on about the fourth and there has been no rain in it in a season, and she knows what a crop in the wrong month costs because she has paid it, and she is not going to tell him what it costs in his yard because it is not his figure to pay.** **He asks her no question in about an hour and the count of the questions he has asked her in this holding's book is nil.** They walk the eleven hundred yards, a cut channel about two feet wide, a fall of about a foot in the whole length, about one in a thousand, and the figures against the bottom of it are four inches, four inches, and four and a half. **At the stone head and a shut field gate there is about nine inches of water standing against the inside of it and the ground on the far side is green.** The two options are worked out on two boards and both are entered and neither is improved: put boards in the cut and five hundred and seventy-five hundredweight a day goes onto a middle that came down a foot and one inch in eight mornings, with no unit and nobody able to say what that does; or leave the cut open and it goes onto nine acres of Marden's low ground in the fourth month behind a gate nobody has opened. **No choice is made in this chapter and the hour is entered.** **The man of about thirty-five of an Assembly in the Thornwild is at the stone head and says that for about ninety yards the channel runs along the edge of ground nobody has spoken for, and does not say whose, and says a man who knows is a man with a reason.** The count of numbers this holding has handed out that it cannot check is two and stays two, and he says out loud that he is not going to make a third out of a number he cannot check. **The count of things written not known against goes to five, and the fifth is the figure that launder carried before the eleventh of the first month, which is in none of the four books and whose unit is not known and may not be supplied.** At about the seventh hour he writes eleven lines in the cart shed about what nine acres of winter wheat does when it comes up green in the fourth month, and does not read them to anybody, and gives the reason on the back of the sheet.

**255 — *The Word Is Not The Same As Asking*** (day 461, a Friday) — **THE BATCH CLIMAX. ZERO `Entered ...` BLOCKS.**
The man of about fifty tells the three other bodies of households at the node at about the second hour of the morning and Marden is not there, because Marden is the fourth line and the fourth line is empty. **The reader of this body has not slept and the count of nights this holding's book records that he has not slept through in four years is twelve.** At about the second hour of the afternoon a yard at Errow Reach has about nineteen people in it, three of the four bodies and the woman of about thirty-eight of Marden and the man of the north row with the cough at the **twenty-first** of his yards and not asked, and he said nothing and that is the fifth time in about four weeks. **He tells her what he is going to decide BEFORE he decides it, and the reason he is telling her first is that she is the one it is going to happen to and about eleven people in that yard are not.** The cut stays open, no boards, no boards this season, and the standing rule is not used and he says so out loud so that nobody can say afterwards that he reached for it. **The reason is arithmetic and he has got nothing else: the middle is about four feet across, nine feet off the hollow, a foot and one inch lower over eight mornings, nobody has ever put a unit on that fall, and if the cut is shut then five hundred and seventy-five hundredweight a day goes onto it and there is nowhere lower for it to go in this holding, and putting boards in a two-foot cut is easy and takes a quarter of an hour with two people and is the correct thing to do with the things he has in his hand, and he is not going to do it.** **She says one sentence: *Then it is my gate and it is not yours, and you have told me, and that is not the same thing as asking me, and I know that it is not the same thing as asking me, and I am going to tell you what I am going to do about it, and you are not going to be here when I do it.*** **HE HEARS HER AND HE DOES NOT ANSWER HER AND HE DOES NOT CHANGE IT, and the whole of that is entered in his own hand: THE COUNT OF QUESTIONS AVAILABLE TO A ROOM IN THIS HOLDING AND NOT TAKEN WAS NINE AND IT IS TEN; HEARING A PERSON IS NOT ASKING A PERSON; HE KNEW THE DIFFERENCE BEFORE SHE SAID IT AND HAD IT IN A BOOK ON THE EIGHTH AND READ IT BACK TWICE; BEING RIGHT IS THE WHOLE OF WHAT HE IS. The number was not said out loud and the promise made in a yard in the second month held.** The decision is said once, in the plain voice: it stays open, and nobody is to put anything in that gap this season, and a man who wants to put something in it is to come and stand in that yard and say so in about nineteen people and not do it in the night. The woman of about thirty-eight of Marden goes down the shoulder at about the fourth hour with nobody. **At about the eleventh hour of the morning, before any of that, he went out to the west sill with a rule in his right hand and stood about four minutes, and the water moved about once in every four or five seconds, and HE DID NOT PUT THE RULE AGAINST IT, and the reason is that a man with two figures of his own and one of an office's has got a comparison, and a man who has got a comparison and goes back to the sill for a third figure is a man who has stopped reading and started wanting.** At about the seventh hour he reads the sixth line of the sheet by lamplight, cannot work out the date of the next window, writes it as the sixth thing not known, and writes two more lines and reads them back: **nineteen days from this morning is the thirtieth of this month, the fourth line comes round on the twelfth, the sixteenth, the twentieth, the twenty-fourth and the twenty-eighth, and on the thirtieth the standing for the month is read out in about twenty-five people at nine hundred yards off and he will be standing in that yard; the water on the low ground of Marden will be a great deal further along than it is tonight and he is the only person in this fen who has said that sentence out loud, and he is going to have to be in a yard in about four days saying it, and he has said four things out loud in a yard and got wrong and does not know what the fifth is going to be, and a schedule that says it will be reviewed is not a schedule that says it will stop.**
